### 2026-10-12 - A registered code is a name: Seattle's 27 buildings and Prague's 14 stations go back (owner)

- **Decided by the owner, 2026-10-12** ("yes push as built ... you can fix
  the seattle/prague issues here"), after Cleanup reported that the
  code-plus-type rule had taken 83 and 32 rows where it had said "about 20"
  and "six". The rule landed as built at `f7867f86`; this entry is the fix.
  - **A landmark that carries wikidata, wikipedia, `heritage` or `ref:nrhp`
    keeps a coded name** (`REGISTERED_KEYS`, `pipeline/place_index.py`). The
    register knows the object by that label, so it is a name there. A bare
    type word still goes whatever the object carries.
  - **Measured against `f7867f86` on all 19 indexes: 41 rows back, none
    removed, 17 indexes unchanged.** Seattle 27 (the Naval Air Station and
    Fort Lawton buildings and the Locks' four operating houses, each with a
    National Register number), Prague 14 (each station has its own wikidata
    item). None of Surrey's 82 parcels and not Busan's park lot carry any of
    the four keys, so they stay out.
  - **Net of both entries: 149 rows out of 16 indexes.** Seattle's and
    Prague's maps were re-rendered a second time.
  - **Cleanup's reading, not the owner's words:** "fix" was taken to mean
    putting these two sets back, the only issue raised about them.

### 2026-10-12 - Korean brand names may appear in public chain tables; the Chicago ranking fix and small open items (owner)

- **Korean brand names (owner: "yes tell analytics", on Cleanup's
  recommendation).** A brand name from the Korean small-business register
  may appear in a public piece when (1) it is a chain, recurring across
  several premises by the chain table's own threshold, since a one-shop
  trade name there can be the owner's own name, and (2) the piece carries
  the register's credit and distorts no fact (row AN-5,
  `docs/commercial_use_positions.md`). Each public piece still needs the
  owner's go-ahead; Daegu stays out. Analytics was told.
- **Chicago's "Lake Shore Dr" (owner: "fix"; then "fold chicago into wave
  1's landing").** Cause, measured by running the rendered map's own search
  script on the index: the 5-point bonus for the typed street type went to
  every kind, so the Lake Shore Drive Synagogue scored -36.4 against the
  Drive's -35.9. The bonus now goes to streets, crossings and junctions
  only; the eight Drives lead and "Art Institute", "Damen and North", "Clark
  and Lake" and Seattle's "NE 45th St" rank as before. Held on local branch
  `chicago-rank-fix` until wave 1's landing re-renders the 19 searched maps.
- **Osaka's labels, checked on the deployed page**: 34 labels at a 343 px
  map, no overlapping pair, measured from the label boxes.
- **Two stray extracts deleted from the main checkout's root** on the
  owner's word, each byte-identical to its copy in
  `data/_basemap_build/data/sources/`.
- **Search behaviour** (the four-letter gate, streets after other kinds,
  the folded places group): "good for now" (owner).
- **The outside net's rule changes on Vancouver**: two Surrey parcels the
  pattern misses are held for wave 1's landing (`PLAN.md`).

### 2026-10-12 - Place search gets a defined area: the scope rule, the outline on the map, the credit in the bottom line, the boundary fetched first (owner)

- **Decided by the owner, 2026-10-12**, on a proposal relayed by the wave 1
  search session and Cleanup's recommendations: "1. yes part of rollout. 2.
  before [wave 1's re-render]. 3. approved"; "cost is acceptable"; "B,
  wording approved, drop the area line on no-result"; "yes force the
  boundary first".
  - **The scope rule.** An index covers exactly the municipalities its map
    covers: the boundary the businesses are cut to and, on a regional map,
    every municipality a drawn station sits in; a station on unincorporated
    land adds the smallest named area that holds it; nothing wider without
    the owner's word (`config.PLACE_AREA_KEPT`). Check H stays the floor;
    `scripts/check_place_scope.py` is the ceiling. Measured on the 19 built
    cities: every municipality holds a station, none needed a kept entry.
    Of wave 1's 34 only Buffalo (planned as Erie County) and Miami (planned
    as Miami-Dade County, seven stations on unincorporated land) change;
    Miami goes to the owner as its own proposal.
  - **The outline.** One merged outline per city in the index ("area":
    names and rings), exterior rings only, simplified by 40 m in the city's
    own projected CRS, parts under 0.2% left out. Drawn dashed only while
    the search box is open. **Measured: 0.6% on the indexes** (143,641
    bytes over 18 cities, about 53 KB gzipped; São Paulo's 21 KB the
    largest; Seoul was fetched after the table).
  - **Wording, approved by the owner:** on no result, "Search covers the
    outlined area only. Zoom out to see it."; in the box, "Search covers
    <municipality> and N more, outlined on the map." (left out when a
    search finds nothing); in the bottom line, "Search index ODbL
    (download, intersections)".
  - **The credit moved (B).** The owner chose it on a local render of both:
    the ODbL notice and the download offer sit in the map's bottom
    attribution line, which wraps to two lines on a phone (28 px at 343 px).
    The page bullet is unchanged: the owner's point was that a bullet
    outside the map is off screen on a phone.
  - **The boundary is the fetch's first query**, and the fetch stops before
    its two heavy queries when a municipality holds no station and has no
    recorded reason, or a boundary does not close. Limit: one too-large
    relation (a county) passes that count; it is caught by reading the
    config, as the skill now says. No hand-off pass between sessions: each
    wave's own queue brings boundary and places.
- **Found on the way, and built: a mirror serving old data.**
  `overpass.kumi.systems` answered on 2026-10-12 from data dated 2026-05-31
  to 2026-07-24. Five of wave 1's main caches and one of London's twelve
  tiles (live; 70,683 elements) carry it. The fetch now refuses a place
  answer over 7 days old (`PLACE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`); boundaries accept any
  date, and all 19 outlines came from that mirror while `overpass-api.de`
  did not answer (from about 23:50Z for over two hours). The owner: "1. yes
  refetch. 2. yes refetch london tile", both when the other mirror returns.
- **Schema number 1** went into the index and intersections file in the same
  re-render, ahead of the first wave where the owner had approved it, so the
  wave's landing needs no second re-render; the id side file is still owed.
- **Cleanup's own slips, for the record:** the first outline query asked for
  `out tags geom` and returned no geometry (refetched); a first queue ran
  unpinned against the unreachable mirror and was stopped.

### 2026-10-13 - The id side file for the travel app, built on the 19 searched cities (owner)

- **Decided by the owner** ("yes build the id side file now"), the second of
  the two additions approved on 2026-10-12 for the travel app; the schema
  number went in with the outline.
  - **What it is.** `outputs/<city>/place_ids.json`, written by the index
    step beside the summary: `rows` and `osm`, two parallel lists, the row's
    position in the index and its OpenStreetMap object ("node/123"), for
    every station, junction, landmark and neighborhood row. It carries
    `schema`, the index's path, the **sha256 of the index file it
    describes**, and the licence name and attribution. Under `outputs/`,
    never `app/static`: no browser fetches it.
  - **What has no id.** Streets (each is many ways merged by name) and
    rivers (several ways under one name): 0 to 277 non-street rows a city,
    Kyoto's 277 the most.
  - **Measured on all 19: no index changed** (the drift check, and git shows
    only the 19 new files). 74,069 rows carry an id; every id is an object
    in its city's cache; 6 rows show a name that is one of two the object
    lists with a semicolon, and are the right object. Sizes 11 KB (Sydney)
    to 273 KB (Tokyo), about 1.6 MB in all.
  - **Held by** `check_place_search.py`, rule I (the hash is this index's,
    rows ascending and in range and never a street, ids well formed), and by
    the place index drift check, which compares every file the step writes.

### 2026-10-13 - A picked street is drawn at its whole length, from a street lines side file; the ring is the failsafe (owner)

- **Asked and decided by the owner** ("can we make searching a street
  without an intersection show the whole length of the street?"; then "yes
  build it as a side file, measure all 19 first"; then, on the rows with no
  line, "ring where line is not possible is a good failsafe, record").
  - **What is built** (branch `street-lines`, on `map-rights`; NOT on
    master, no index rebuilt, no map re-rendered): the index step writes
    `app/static/places/streets/<city>.json`, each street row's ways joined
    end to end and simplified by 10 m in the city's own CRS
    (`place_index.chain_ways`, `street_lines`, `STREET_SIMPLIFY_M`). The
    index names the file under its hash (`"streets"`, a key added, schema
    still 1). The map fetches it at the first street picked and draws the
    line in the ring's colours; the name label stays.
  - **The failsafe.** The ring is drawn first and hidden only once a line is
    on the map, so it stays when the index names no street file, when the
    fetch fails, and for a row with no line.
  - **Why a side file.** Measured on Seattle before building: the shapes are
    larger than the whole index (330 KB gzipped against 230 KB), so in the
    index they would have more than doubled what opening the search costs.
  - **How accurate, as told to the owner: mostly, not totally.** A line is
    what OSM names with exactly the row's name: it stops where the name
    changes (a third of Seattle's street names, 3,707 of 11,117, share a
    base with a direction-word sibling such as Pike Street and East Pike
    Street), at the edge of the search area, and at any gap. Not counted:
    bridges and ramps under their own name.
  - **Measured on the 19 built cities** (the real step, written nowhere):
    15.6 MB raw and 5.8 MB gzipped in all; London 2,841 / 1,094 KB, Mexico
    City 2,732 / 997, São Paulo 2,412 / 870, the smallest Brussels 75 / 29.
    Of about 254,000 street rows, 11 have no line (four in São Paulo, one
    each in seven cities). The delta-integer encoding is 62% of plain
    coordinates' bytes on Seattle.
  - **Tested** on a local Seattle map in the browser: "Pike Street" and
    "Rainier Avenue South" drawn, the ring's two paths hidden, the label
    shown. Not tested: a phone, a failed fetch in a browser.
  - **Held by** `check_place_search.py` rule J (the file is this index's;
    every line inside its street row's extent; selftest 28 of 28).
  - **For future waves:** the `place-street-lines` skill.
  - **Open with the owner:** the line's colour, a "streets" link in the
    credit line, 10 or 20 m for the three largest cities, and the word to
    rebuild the 19.

### 2026-10-13 - The maps' Cities menu under non clickable headers, Japan last (owner)

- **Decided by the owner** ("could we make collapsible region groups within
  the list: or non clickable divider section headers?"; then "non clickable
  headers sounds good"; then "also put japanese cities at the bottom since
  they are the largest group").
  - **Why headers and not collapsing groups.** The menu is the browser's own
    dropdown, 236 cities in one run. A native dropdown takes headers
    (optgroup) and cannot collapse; collapsing means a custom panel inside
    the map, a larger build with more to get right on a phone.
  - **The grouping** (`cities.MENU_GROUPS`, 43 headers): a country in one
    region is one group under its name; a country in several regions is a
    group per region, "Country · Region" ("United States · West", "Japan ·
    Kansai"), because one header over Japan's 94 cities divides nothing.
    The split is this session's choice, told to the owner, not yet answered.
    Japan's 13 groups come last (`MENU_LAST`). `SWITCHER_ORDER` is unchanged.
  - **How.** The app page writes the headers and their link counts in one
    hidden element beside the hidden city links; the map's menu reads both.
    A map rendered before this lists the links plainly, in the new order.
  - **A fault found on the way and fixed:** the menu was built once and kept,
    so one filled while the page had drawn only some links stayed short. It
    is now rebuilt whenever the links or headers have changed.
  - **Tested** on a local Seattle page: 43 headers, 236 options, Seattle
    itself left out. Not tested: an iPhone's picker, Safari on a Mac.
  - **Held** on branch `street-lines`; needs the full re-render and a reboot.

### 2026-10-13 - Street lines: the teal stays, a third credit link, the 19 rebuilt; the picked-line look goes to every map (owner)

  - **The street line keeps the search ring's teal.** Offered against it: a

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: the owner's rulings on keep against drop, personal names, private streets and the typo line; NOT yet built

- **The street-lines batch landed** (master 4d7cf5f9, the owner's "yes sounds
  good"; rebooted by the owner; verified live on Seattle: 43 menu headers, a
  street drawn with its ring hidden, three credit links, the rights tags).
- **Two read-only measures on the 19 built cities came first** (the owner:
  "yes measure first", and "identify any other cases where a positive rule
  should outweigh a negative or vice versa", "e.g. personal names"). Both
  rebuilt every index in memory and matched the committed files (328,692
  rows). Scripts and results: the session scratchpad, `rules_measure/` and
  `rules_precedence/`; the figures below are from them.
- **The owner's words, on the list put to them** ("1" the audit's lean
  tables, "2" personal names, "3" private residential streets, "4" the typo
  line, "5" wave 1's twelve bare-type drops with a Wikidata entry only):
  "2. recommendation approved. 3. drop as ruled, add to list of dropped
  categories (unless one doesn't exist for searches, then we should probably
  start one now and retrofit). 1. all as leaned. 5. drop. 4. short okay".
  Earlier the same day: "famous landmark rule takes priority over others".
- **1. Keep now beats drop** where the object has its OWN Wikipedia article
  and today's drop is: a bare type word (81 rows; not where the article is
  the type's, as with Paris's 69 Édicule Guimard); a privately operated or
  office tag (115; brand rows stay out, the Panama Hotel precedent); a place
  kind the index does not list, for islands, islets and districts (121); a
  pedestrian square drawn as an area (47 of 75; rest areas stay out); an
  estate drawn as an area (46, to match the estate kept as a point); the
  home test tripped by access=private on something that is no home (about
  16); and a small memorial with an article but no Wikidata entry (23).
- **1. Drop now beats keep:** a place or landmark its own tags say is
  demolished, razed, abandoned or not yet open, article or not (266, of
  which about 126 Hong Kong hamlets), "the search must not land where the
  thing is not"; a station that is a business or a bare type (6); a closed
  station (2); a row admitted only by a Wikidata entry shared by three or
  more rows (23).
- **1. Left as they are:** a home with its own article stays out (294; the
  16 non-homes above excepted); everything the audit ranked "looks right".
- **2. Personal names:** statues, busts and public memorials stay; a grave
  or a plaque is kept only when the object has its own Wikipedia article. A
  Wikidata entry alone no longer admits one (258 graves and 241 plaques are
  in on a key today; nothing in the data tells a private person from a
  public one, which is why the article is the test).
- **3. Private residential streets are dropped, as first ruled**: a street
  whose every way is access=private or no on residential or unclassified
  (4,102 rows, 1.6% of streets; Mexico City 1,982, London 1,088). Put to the
  owner with the Paris precedent of 2026-10-11 it departs from (addressed
  private streets kept) and two narrower options; the owner kept the ruling.
  The French named-street exception stands (52 of them) and so does a way's
  own article. **And a list of what search leaves out is to exist**: none
  does for search (`docs/excluded_categories.md` is the businesses'), so one
  is started and the settled classes retrofitted into it.
- **4. The typo line, short form, approved:** "Place names come from
  OpenStreetMap as its contributors wrote them, so a few are misspelt." At
  the end of the search bullet in "Using the map". US spelling applies
  site-wide (2026-10-01), so it lands as "misspelled".
- **5. Wave 1's twelve bare-type drops with a Wikidata entry only stay
  dropped**: the landmark ruling rests on an article of the object's own.
- **Also to build, from the rule measure, as leaned and to be reported row
  by row before landing:** one row a stop for same-mode platform rows only
  (294 of 590; interchanges stay two rows); duplicate junction, place and
  landmark rows merged (417); street names that are descriptions or a type
  plus a code, on minor ways only (89 and 41; 193 real streets such as The
  Avenue stay); rides as stations without the tourism=attraction signal
  alone (14); busways and race tracks out unless the way has its own article
  (126); a bridge's own name from bridge:name (3); the not-open test reading
  opening_date, disused:railway and end_date; the candidate bare type words
  (5 rows here). Not built as rules: castles (a web check is owed), coded
  names the rule misses (by id), zoo contents (by id).
- **Every one of these changes built indexes**, so the rule-change protocol
  applies: built on a branch, the 19 rebuilt, every changed row reported to
  the owner before landing; the typo line and any matcher change re-render
  the searched maps.

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: an extract route beside Overpass, proved on two cities; wave 1 first on the main mirror; streets on a shared border queued (owner)

  `f549e6a8` carries its two scripts, 70 of 70 checks) from

  Wave 1 had put it to the owner as an option; Cleanup recommended building

  Geofabrik's terms were read the same day (licence-read: permitted with
  conditions, ODbL 1.0, no credit to Geofabrik, no written usage policy; its
  request is the smallest region, never the same file twice, no daily full
  copies of large regions).
- **The proof** (a scratch prototype, kept in `data/_osm_extracts/proto/`):
  Tbilisi from `georgia-261008` matched its Overpass caches element for
  element (13,229 street ways, 79,174 street nodes, 2,218 tagged) and its
  index row for row, order aside (5,686). Seattle from `washington-261008`,
  12.5 hours newer than its Overpass cache: 47,809 of 47,810 street ways
  identical; two tag differences are edits made in the gap (checked on the

  way/32544404, neither a recent edit); five landmark rows carry the id of a
  same-name duplicate, because the index keeps the first of two and
  Overpass's order is not reproduced.
- **What matching Overpass took:** each municipality tested on its own,
  never their union; inner rings as holes; a way in by a node strictly
  inside or a line through the interior, never by touching the boundary; a
  relation in by a member that is; `out center` as the middle of the
  bounding box.

  allows it; re-order where places are fetched from if the route proves
  faster (to be recommended from measured timings, not yet decided); build
  it "with proper structure/skills" so it can be relayed to other projects
  that use Overpass; enlist DeepSeek in it. A build agent has these on
  branch `place-extract`.
- **Streets on a shared border, the owner: "yes add shared border streets
  to queue where you said to".** Overpass leaves out a street that runs
  exactly along the line between two included municipalities (60 street
  ways in Seattle, 148th Avenue Northeast among them), since it lies in

  the extract route is in production; it changes every built regional
  index, so it goes through the changed-row report like any rule change.
  Until then the extract route matches Overpass and leaves them out.
- **The search-rules landing is the priority** (the owner: "can we make the

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: the owner's two fixes to the changed-row report (three places closed for works kept, two counties by Prague dropped)

- **The owner approved the changed rows with two fixes; both are by id, and five rows change, no others.** The 19 indexes now hold 323,407 rows (Paris 9,227, Prague 10,790, Seattle 13,113; the other 16 untouched). `changed_rows.md` and `.json` are regenerated.
- **Kept by id, through a NEW config list, `PLACE_CLOSED_FOR_WORKS`: the Musée Yves Saint Laurent (Paris), the Seattle Public Library's University Branch and South Park Playground (Seattle).** What tripped the not-yet-open test: opening_date=2027-09 on the museum, building=construction on the library, construction=yes on the playground. No tag separates them from a place not yet built: three different tags, none of historic, heritage or a start date, and they are the only three not-yet-open drops on the 19, so a rule would be fitted to three rows. No by-id keep existed for this (`PLACE_VISITABLE` is for homes and has its own evidence fields, `PLACE_REBUILT` is for streets by name), so the list is new: OSM id and why, read only by the not-yet-open class (a demolished object named there still goes), and an id that has left the cache stops the step. Rejected: reading opening_hours=closed or operational_status=closed, carried by one of the three.
- **Dropped by id in Prague's `PLACE_DROPPED`: okres Praha-východ and okres Praha-západ.** What admitted them: place=district with an article of their own, the district keep. Not tightened by rule: Prague's own ten districts carry the same tags (boundary=administrative, admin_level=5), and a test of the area would need the boundary geometry inside the row rules. The district keep now adds no row on the 19; it stays for other cities.
- **Selftest 80 cases, 55 rule breaks, all caught.**

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: the owner's rulings of 2026-10-13 built on a branch, the 19 indexes rebuilt, every changed row reported; NOT landed

- **Built the rulings of "Place search: the owner's rulings on keep against drop, personal names, private streets and the typo line" in `pipeline/place_index.py`, rebuilt the 19 searched indexes from their caches and reported every changed row; nothing is landed and no map is rendered.** Branch `search-rules`, cut from 89c3ff24. The 19 indexes go from 328,692 rows to 323,406 and from 536,583 intersections to 530,756; index files 26.27 MB to 25.86 MB raw, street files 15.63 MB to 15.44 MB. The full list, a line a row with its OSM id and deciding tags, is `data/_place_rules_2026-10-13/build/changed_rows.md` (and `.json`), written by `build/report.py` from an in-memory rebuild compared with the committed indexes row by row (`build/harness.py`); every changed row carries the rule that logged it, none is unattributed. `check_place_search.py` fails rule A on all 19 (the maps carry the old index hash until the one re-render at landing) and nothing else; `check_place_index_drift.py`, `check_place_scope.py` and `check_place_unopened.py` pass. The baseline was proved first: before any change, all 19 committed indexes matched the code.
- **Built counts against the figures in the ruling, where they differ and why.** Private residential streets 4,047 (ruling 4,102 gross, 4,041 net of the French lead and of ways with wikidata or wikipedia: the built exception is an article of the way's own, so six streets with a Wikidata entry alone go). Busways and raceways 124 of 126 (not traced row by row; a way's own article is the only exception built). Described minor-way street names 124 of 130 (89 and 41; a minor way with wikidata or wikipedia is exempt, as the minor-way rule already exempts it; not traced row by row). Tram platforms 293 of 294 (one Hong Kong platform went first as a closed stop). Duplicates 404 of 417: junctions 226 of 226, places 91 of 96, landmarks 87 of 95 (rows another rule took first; river rows are never merged). Rides 12 of 14 (the two the measure found only by an operator's name are not built). Stations that are a business or a bare type 6 of 6; closed stations 2 of 2. Graves 215 dropped of 258 in on a key, plaques 224 of 241; 39 stay as undecidable by tag and the rest have an article of their own or another tag that holds them. A Wikidata entry shared by three or more rows 14 of 23 (counted after the duplicate merges, and the object an article is named after owns it). Not there 124 of 266, read narrowly (below). Kept on an article of the object's own: bare type 3 of 81 (69 are the Édicule Guimard, the others carry a shared article, a section, or the type's own article), private operator or office 96 of 115 (shared articles, and rows another rule drops), islands, islets and districts 111 of 121 (archipelagos not built), pedestrian areas 39 of 47, estate areas 38 of 46, private access 11 of about 16, small memorials 6 of 23. Candidate bare type words 4 rows of 5 (Seattle's Quad keeps its row on its own article).
- **Decided, as the narrower reading and FLAGGED for the owner: "demolished, razed, abandoned or not yet open" is read from the object's own tags, not from any lifecycle prefix on any key.** The measure's 266 counted every `disused:`, `abandoned:`, `demolished:`, `proposed:` and `construction:` prefix on a feature key, and end dates. Built: a plain flag is the object's own; a prefixed key counts only where the object has no live amenity, tourism, leisure, natural or man_made key (London's St John Zachary is a garden on a demolished church's site); a feature live and prefixed alike is kept and listed (eight Hong Kong parks tagged leisure=park beside construction:leisure=park); an abandoned object with a historic tag is kept as a historic site (Hong Kong's pillboxes and Shing Mun Redoubt, Marseille's batteries); a not-yet-open object that is historic, listed or has a start date behind it is kept as a rebuilding (the Centre Pompidou); `disused`, `was:` and end dates are not read. 124 rows go (105 in Hong Kong, 104 of them places, former villages for the most part); 62 are kept and listed. Rejected: the measured test, which took fountains with a disused drinking tap and artworks on a former fountain. To be ruled on by the owner: Prague's razed horse railway (seven rows, with an article; the 2b audit had put it back), three places closed for works that the tags show as not yet open (the Musée Yves Saint Laurent, a Seattle library branch and a playground), and the 62 kept rows.
- **Decided: the fame test is read from tags as follows, and what the tags cannot tell is left as it was.** Not the object's own: an article named by a section or twice in one tag, one equal to the object's subject, brand or operator article or item, and one another object in the cache carries, unless the object is the one the article is named after. A grave or a plaque is named for its subject, so its article is its own only where the title says tomb, plaque, monument or the like; an article under the row's own name is the subject's. Any other memorial under its subject's name is undecidable. 39 graves and plaques stay as they were (Seoul's fourteen royal tombs tagged with their kings' articles among them) and 54 candidate keeps stay out, all listed. Rejected: guessing from the name whether a person is public.
- **Decided: an island or islet with its own article is a landmark row, a district a neighborhood row.** The index's kinds are fixed and the search labels a place row "Neighborhood". Two districts qualify, both counties that border Prague and not part of it: flagged.
- **Decided: access=private keeps a row out as a home unless it is the only home sign, the object has its own article and it is no garden, park or palace.** Eleven rows come in (libraries, a university building, monuments behind a fence, theatres); 45 gardens and palaces with an article stay out as undecidable by tag.
- **Decided: one street-level reading for the three street rules.** A street is dropped only when every one of its ways fails the rule, so no street changes its drawn length. Rejected: skipping single ways, which would have shortened 71 streets that share a name with a busway.
- **Decided: a merge keeps the member nearest the group's middle, the lower OSM id on a tie, and takes in the others' names.** The row's id in `place_ids.json` is that member's.
- **Decided: a closed station is counted (`station_skipped_closed`) and is not added to the watch file of unopened stations**, which is for stations not yet open. No new unopened station appears on the 19.
- **Four new rows carry a leak word and are reviewed by name; one reviewed name left.** `PLACE_REVIEWED` gained Hôtel de Castries and Hôtel de Matignon (Paris), Hôtel Errera (Brussels) and HMS Wellington (London), each a government building or a ship with its own article, in on the office ruling; Chicago's "Site of the Sauganash Hotel/Wigwam" was removed, since its plaque row went with the plaque rule.
- **Wave 1's four additions.** construction=yes is read on any station or stop (no row on the 19). usage=tourism is in the ride rule and takes 11 of the 12 rides dropped. No highway way tagged historic=roman_road is a landmark row on the 19, so nothing is proposed from this data; the same pattern exists for historic=railway on a street in Prague (two rows, kept). The plaque rule and the shared-item rule take the pavement plates and subject-item plaques found (Paris's fourteen "Hommage à Arago" plates share one article); a threshold of two would drop 48 more rows (measured only, listed).
- **A selftest for the index rules, new:** `python pipeline/place_index.py --selftest` (78 cases on a made-up city, in `check_all.py`); `scripts/place_index_breaks.json` breaks 53 rules in a copy and all 53 are caught.
- **The typo sentence is in `app/components.py`** (`render_map_help`), the owner's wording with US spelling. An `app/` change: it lands with the batch and needs the reboot.
- **`docs/place_search_exclusions.md` is the internal list of what search leaves out**, every settled class with its rule, where it lives and its date.
- **Not done:** no map rendered (the landing's one re-render); the matcher change for a correctly typed word finding a misspelled name was not sized; `pipeline/drift_check.py` was not run (it runs city steps against the shared data folder).
- **PROPOSAL for review time, a DRAFT, the owner's wording to settle: the section for the What Is Excluded page.**

  > ## What the map's search leaves out
  >
  > The search box finds places, not businesses. It reads a list of streets, intersections, stations, neighborhoods and landmarks taken from OpenStreetMap for the area each map covers. Some places are left off that list on purpose.
  >
  > **Homes.** A house, an apartment building or a private garden is not in the search, even a well-known one. A former home is included when it is open to visitors as a museum.
  >
  > **Businesses.** A shop, a hotel, a restaurant or an office is not a search result. A historic building is included for the building, not for the business inside it.
  >
  > **People.** A grave or a plaque is included only when it has its own Wikipedia article. Statues and public memorials are included. Stumbling stones, which mark a person's last home, are never included.
  >
  > **Private streets.** A street closed to the public is left out. So are driveways, parking aisles, bus-only roads and race tracks.
  >
  > **Places that are not there.** A building that was demolished, a village that was abandoned and a station that has closed or has not opened yet are left out. The search should not take you to something that is gone.
  >
  > **Names that are not names.** A park labeled only "Park" or a path labeled "Footpath 12" is left out, because the label describes the thing and does not name it.
  >
  > **Small or repeated things.** Wayside shrines, boundary stones and museum exhibits are left out. A tram stop with two platforms is one result, not two.
  >
  > Place names come from OpenStreetMap as its contributors wrote them, so a few are misspelled.

### 2026-10-13 - Place search from regional extracts (branch place-extract)

- **Built the extract route as two files: a general reader and this project's
  caller.** `pipeline/osm_extract.py` holds everything another project could
  lift (region choice from the server's `index-v1.json`, the download and its
  conduct, area polygons with holes, the area tests, a parser for Overpass
  union statements, the three-pass read, Overpass's output shapes);
  `pipeline/place_extract_fetch.py` reads `place_index_fetch.py`'s three
  queries as written and writes the three place caches. Rejected: a second
  copy of the query as Python predicates (the prototype's form), because a
  union line changed in one file would not reach the other. The caller
  instead parses the union text and exits when a query's shape outside its
  union changes. `scripts/place_extract_compare.py` is the proof's report.
- **Proved it with the production code on four cities, extracts dated
  2026-10-08T20:21Z against Overpass caches of the same day.** Tbilisi:
  13,229 street ways, 79,174 street nodes and 2,218 tagged elements identical
  in id, tags, node lists and position; index 5,686 rows identical. Seattle
  (11 municipalities): 47,809 of 47,810 street ways identical, 2 tag
  differences both edited after the Overpass date (node/29546940 at 09:47Z,
  way/521303400 at 18:20Z), 2 objects only in the extract. Brussels: 5,752
  street ways and 1,389 tagged elements identical, 2 ways only in the
  extract. Kyoto (area by tag filter): 5,739 street ways and 13,570 tagged
  elements identical apart from 3 tag edits dated after the Overpass date, 2
  ways only in the extract, one of them edited at 17:52Z. No element of any
  Overpass answer was absent from the extract's.
- **Found the cause class of the unexplained objects, not the mechanism, and
  did not tune for them.** Five unedited objects in four cities are in the
  extract's answer and not Overpass's, and every one lies on the boundary:
  Seattle's way/428075842 has one node 3.6 mm inside Kent's line (under the
  1e-7 degree grid) and three on it; way/32544404 shares 16 of 19 nodes with
  Redmond's boundary and cuts up to 2.5 m inside between two of them;
  Brussels' way/14193701 clips a corner for 2 cm with every node outside and
  way/35272078 runs 0.3 m inside between boundary nodes; Kyoto's
  way/45249938 runs 32 m through a corner with all 17 nodes outside. No
  boundary way, node or relation of Seattle's eleven was edited after
  Overpass's areas date (2026-10-07T10:19Z), which rules out the
  edited-boundary reading. Of the 12 street ways the line test alone admits
  in the four cities Overpass returned 9, and neither length inside nor
  depth separates the 9 from the 3 (1.7 m inside returned, 32 m not), so no
  threshold was added. How Overpass decides these is not established.
- **Changed rule 5 on a measured difference: a center's latitude is averaged
  the way Overpass holds it.** The prototype's comparison allowed 1.5e-7
  degrees and so hid that `round((lo + hi) / 2, 7)` printed 121 of Tbilisi's
  772 center latitudes one last digit off (389 of the 772 are exact halves in
  the 8th decimal). Averaging latitudes as doubles offset by 91 degrees, and
  longitudes plainly, matched all 772; it then matched every center of
  Seattle, Brussels and Kyoto with no further change. The reason given for
  the offset (Overpass's stored form) is from memory of its source, not read
  today.
- **Chose ascending id as the one written order, and measured what it does to
  the index.** Street ways, their nodes, then nodes, ways and relations, each
  by id. Overpass's `qt` order is not reproduced. Re-ordering the Overpass
  caches themselves this way changes 0 of Tbilisi's 5,686 rows, 5 of
  Seattle's 13,426 (which of two same-name landmark ways is kept), 0 of
  Brussels' 2,059 and 158 of Kyoto's 9,331 (155 junctions whose English
  alias comes from a differently capitalised street name, 3 landmarks). A
  city refetched by the extract route will therefore show such rows as
  changed against its Overpass-built index.
- **Decided what the fetch does with caches and provenance.** A cache on disk
  is never written over without `--force`, whoever wrote it, and a kept
  cache's provenance entry is left as it was rather than re-recorded as
  kept. `--out DIR` writes all three elsewhere and records nothing (the
  proof's route). `PLACE_SPLIT` is unused and said so. `PLACE_AREA` as tag
  filters is answered from boundary and multipolygon relations only; an
  area made from a closed way is taken by its id (2400000000 + the way's
  id) and outlined as a way with geometry; a relation's own tags are never
  consulted when it is named by id.
- **Set the download's conduct in code, with no override.** A dated file once
  downloaded and since removed is refused by a ledger
  (`data/_osm_extracts/downloads.json`); the only way past is to edit the
  ledger by hand. One download at a time on the machine
  (`osm_extract.DownloadLock`), so two runs sharing a regional file are
  safe. Defaults: `place_extract_fetch.py --max-mb 1000`, the lookup's
  `--max-mb 800`. Updating a region by diffs is not built.
- **Gave the area lookup a second source, and left Overpass the default.**
  `scripts/place_search_area_lookup.py --source extract` reads
  `osm_extract.boundaries_holding` from the smallest region holding all of a
  city's stations; a city whose smallest covering region has subregions is
  left for a person. A held extract of any age is used. A relation the
  extract holds only part of (the country, sometimes the state) is not
  answered and is listed in the cache. Added `--cache-root` so a trial
  leaves a city's raw folder untouched; the proof's caches are under
  `data/_osm_extracts/build_test/lookup/`, none under any `data/<city>/raw/`.
- **Confirmed both official-code tag keys on real data.** `ref:INSEE` tied
  Strasbourg (67482) and Mulhouse (68224) in alsace-261012; `IBGE:GEOCODIGO`
  tied Porto Alegre (4314902) in sul-261012, and the six relations chosen
  carry exactly the six codes of that config's `SCOPE_CODES`. Each key was
  checked in one region only. Belgian municipalities carry `ref:INS` (Antwerp
  11002, Liège 62063, equal to those configs' NIS codes): not added to
  `CODE_TAGS`, a change for the owner.
- **Lookup results on eight cities with no search, all right by their own
  configs:** Tacoma (relation 8348295, level 8), Kobe (900329, level 7,
  `MUNICIPALITY`), Otsu (722196), Antwerp (59518), Liège (1681788),
  Strasbourg (71033), Mulhouse (38246), Porto Alegre and its five neighbors
  (242397, 242399, 242471, 242574, 242616, 242778). On built cities the
  returned set held every relation `PLACE_AREA` names (Tbilisi 1 of 1,
  Seattle 11 of 11, Brussels 1 of 1, Kyoto 1 of 1). Brussels' stations also
  sit in Ixelles and Saint-Josse-ten-Noode at level 8, which its
  `PLACE_AREA` does not name: for the owner, not changed.
- **Did not call DeepSeek.** The `deepseek-delegate` skill requires the
  owner's yes before every call to its wrapper, and a build agent cannot
  obtain one. The offline compatibility check passed (claude 2.1.296, 9 of 9
  settings). Nothing was sent.
- **PROPOSAL for review time (a reference-doc sentence no template covers):**
  the bullet "A second route to the same OpenStreetMap data: regional
  extracts from download.geofabrik.de" added to `docs/data_sources.md`'s
  place search section, written from the 2026-10-13 licence read. Also new:
  the `osm-extract` skill and the alternative named in `place-search-city`
  step 3, which does not make the extract route the default.

### 2026-10-13 - Several regional extracts read as one (branch multi-region)

- **Built the owner's "proper fix" for a search area that crosses a region
  border: the reader takes the few small regions that together cover the
  area and reads them as one.** `osm_extract.choose_regions` keeps ONE region
  whenever one covers the area and either nothing else meets it or its file
  is under the cap; otherwise it takes the smallest set by total bytes, at
  most four regions (`SET_MAX_REGIONS`), each under the cap. Sizes come from
  HEAD requests, so nothing is downloaded to learn a size. Rejected: raising
  the cap to the parent region (us-northeast is 1,803,892,540 bytes for a
  city that needs 512,803,904), and merging files on disk with a second tool
  (a new dependency and a second copy of the data). Four was chosen as the
  limit because Philadelphia needs two and no measured city more than two;
  an area needing five is nearer a country than a city and is left for a
  person. Files: `pipeline/osm_extract.py`, `pipeline/place_extract_fetch.py`,
  `scripts/place_search_area_lookup.py`.
- **Decided a set must be ONE snapshot, bound 0 seconds
  (`SET_MAX_SPREAD_S`).** Measured on the 23 extracts held that day: every
  file of one daily run states the same timestamp to the second (19 at
  2026-10-12T20:21:35Z, 4 at 2026-10-08T20:21:06Z), and two runs are a day
  apart, so no bound between zero and a day separates anything. A day was
  rejected because an object moved across the cut between two snapshots can
  be in neither file, which no comparison of copies detects. `download_set`
  therefore completes a set at the date most of it is already held at (the
  server keeps several days of dated files), which is also the fewest
  downloads; every file still goes through `download_extract`, one at a
  time, never one in the ledger.
- **Decided what to do with two copies of one object, and refused to guess.**
  An extract keeps whole every way and relation that crosses its cut, so the
  files of a set overlap. Equal copies: the first is kept. A copy holding
  fewer of its nodes' positions: the fuller one, since a cut-short copy is
  incomplete, not different. Two full copies that differ: the read stops and
  names the object and both files (`osm_extract._Once`). Rejected: taking the
  higher version, because files of one snapshot cannot differ and a
  difference means the set is not what its timestamps claim. On the real
  files every shared object was an equal copy: 122 for Philadelphia (63
  boundary member ways, 35 ways, 20 relations, 2 nodes, the boundary relation
  and one member node).
- **Proved the set on Philadelphia with real files, and found the second
  file added nothing.** us/new-jersey (164,191,466 bytes) + us/pennsylvania
  (348,612,438), both dated 261012. The outline assembled from the two files
  equals the Overpass outline cache on disk (relation 188022, 168 members,
  geometry, tags and bounds identical; Overpass data 2026-10-13T06:19:51Z).
  The answer was 27,683 street ways, 153,170 street nodes, 3,420 tagged
  elements and 458 extra. Read from pennsylvania ALONE with the scope checks
  off, the answer is the same, byte for byte: the 0.0002% of the area inside
  New Jersey's polygon is river. **The acceptance bar set for this work,
  nothing in the Overpass answer missing from the set's, was NOT tested for
  the two place caches: Philadelphia has no Overpass place cache on disk
  (only its outline), us-northeast was not on disk, and no Overpass request
  was allowed.** 346.8 s with both downloads, peak 1,625 MB (pennsylvania
  alone: 118 s, 1.05 GB); the Korean sets peaked at 2,136 MB after a first
  try in the same process (south-korea alone: 0.97 GB). A set's memory is
  more than its largest file's alone; the cause was not looked into.
- **Found Washington D.C. is not a multi-region city under its current
  config.** `PLACE_AREA` on branch search-wave1 is the District's relation
  alone (162069), which us/district-of-columbia (21,028,680 bytes, held
  since another session's download) covers by itself; Maryland's and
  Virginia's polygons overlap it by 0.5% and 4.0% but the one region is under
  the cap, so it is taken. The changed reader's answer from that file is
  byte-equal to the caches the unchanged code wrote that day (16,074 street
  ways, 113,347 street nodes, 1,892 tagged, 630 extra): the single-region
  path did not move. A regional D.C. area with Maryland and Virginia
  municipalities would be a three-file set (not downloaded, not tested).
- **Decided an area reaching past its region's polygon is one of two cases,
  told apart by the index (asked for by the rollout session after Gimpo and
  Incheon stopped).** The part outside lies in ANOTHER region: that region
  joins the set, however small the part. The part lies in NO other region
  (open sea): the region is accepted as water-side overflow when the part is
  at most 1% of the area (`OVERFLOW_MAX_SHARE`) and every boundary closed
  from the files read; the size is printed and recorded in the cache's
  source line and `_extract.stats.overflow`. A region larger than the one
  read (its country, its continent) does not count as "another region".
  Measured: Gimpo 0.04 km2 outside south-korea (0.014% of 294.8 km2) and
  Incheon 8.82 km2 (0.089% of 9,906.4 km2), and BOTH lie inside north-korea's
  polygon (the Han estuary), so both are sets, not overflow: north-korea
  (92,675,862 bytes, downloaded once) + south-korea (held). Gimpo: 4,904
  street ways, 75,007 street nodes, 422 tagged, 136 extra, 21 objects in both
  files, all equal. Incheon: 15,971 street ways, 126,760 street nodes, 2,508
  tagged, 529 extra, 98 in both files, all equal. Neither city has an
  Overpass place cache, so counts only; each set's answer equals
  south-korea's alone byte for byte (scope checks off). One percent is
  ten times the largest measured mismatch; **no overflow over open sea has
  been measured, so the bound is untested on a real city** and is to be
  re-measured on the first one accepted.
- **Decided the command chooses again, once, when the region was chosen from
  stations and the area then read reaches into another region.** Without an
  outline cache the area is unknown until the boundary is read; the run
  stops with `AreaPastRegion` and `place_extract_fetch.cli` repeats the
  choice from the area as read. Rejected: probing every city's boundary
  before choosing (a cost on every city for a rare case). Gimpo's first try
  cost 10.7 s.
- **Added the refusal the rollout session asked for: a cache KEPT on disk is
  held to the current `config.PLACE_AREA`.** The area comes from the cache's
  provenance entry when that entry names the file, else from the query the
  cache carries; ids in another order are the same area. A different area
  exits non-zero naming both, before anything is written; a cache recording
  no area is kept with a printed warning.
- **Changed the area lookup: a city whose stations span two regions is read
  from the set, where it was left for a person.** It uses the same
  `choose_regions`; a relation cut at one file's edge is whole across the
  set. This also means a city spanning regions is now answered from its one
  covering region when that file is under the cap, which the earlier rule
  ("never from a file with subregions") did not allow. The lookup's cap is
  1000 MB, as the fetch's (raised on search-rollout the same night).

- **Built the shared-border rule in the extract route only, as a named
  option that is off by default, and turned it on for street ways.**

  named, an element that fails every single-boundary test and passes rule 3
  against the boundaries taken together (their union, holes kept).
  `place_extract_fetch.SHARED_BORDER_UNIONS = ("streets",)` is the one
  switch; `--overpass-exact` turns it off for a run. The main cache's
  `_extract.shared_border` block lists every way admitted with the two
  municipalities it lies between, and the provenance entry's `how` repeats
  the count. Rejected: testing the union for every element (the

  answer and a caller must be able to list what the departure added.

- **The owner ruled on the six questions of the entry below ("1 i agree with you. 2. yes agreed. 3. keep as per-city with reasoning. 4. keep by name. 5. stays. 6. yes stop area with your rule", relayed by the Cleanup session) and each was built on `search-rules-2`.** The 19 live indexes now go from 323,407 rows to 322,843 (322,788 before these rulings) and from 530,756 intersections to 529,733; wave 1's 33, with its by-id drops applied, from 311,182 to 307,474. Both reports under `data/_place_rules_2026-10-13/batch2/` were regenerated; no row is unattributed. Selftest 136 cases, 109 breaks, all caught.
- **(1) A register entry stays on a Wikidata entry of its own as well as an article.** Own: no other object of the city's cache carries the entry, and it is not the object's subject's, brand's or operator's (`own_item`). That is what catches an entry for a class of thing, which sits on several objects (Seoul's tanks under their model's entry; Seattle's three lock ways under the Locks'). Not read for historic=engine, where a lone example would carry its model's entry unshared. Back on the 19: Pacific Tower, Naval Reserve Armory Building and Canoe House (Seattle); 15 rows back in wave 1. **The Ballard Locks are kept by id**: the dam (way/32823954) carries neither an article nor an entry, and the three lock ways share one of each (Q5769218, en:Ballard Locks), so none is one way's own. No existing setting keeps a row against a rule (`PLACE_REVIEWED` is check E's list for leak words, and fails on a name that carries none), so `config.PLACE_KEPT` was added: an OSM id and why, read by the register-entry and bare-type rules only; an id not in the cache stops the step. **None of the five Brooklyn Navy Yard rows a wave 1 reader kept comes back**: the historic district, the Navy Yard Center, the Barrier Forts Monument, Dry Dock 1 and Building 77 carry the district's register number and neither an article nor an entry.
- **(2) A street's way is dropped as a landmark only where a street row bears the name the landmark row would have, bridge:name leading; the first batch's bridge:name rule is back.** Back on the 19: Aurora Bridge and Montlake Bridge (Seattle), Seoul's way/471457870 and Sydney's Law Lawns, two more than the two expected, each with no street row of its name; 23 still drop. Wave 1: 19 back, 57 still drop.
- **(3) `config.PLACE_KEEP_INTERCHANGES = True`, set for Hong Kong with the owner's reason**: all 44 rows stay. London's six and Tokyo's one still go, and Manchester's 13.
- **(4) London's "Greenway" stays by id in `PLACE_KEPT`** (way/120317147, relation/17674520); the word stays on the list.
- **(5) A disused place of worship with an article or a Wikidata entry of its own stays.** Back: Montreal's way/46867692. Washington's two disused temples carry neither and still go.
- **(6) A stop-area relation stays where no other row carries its station.** The cache holds no members, so the test is position and name: another station row within 150 m, one of its folded name within 400 m, or one sharing its Wikidata entry. Kept: Washington's "H Street & 3rd Street Northeast" (relation/11491459) and "Benning Road & Oklahoma Avenue Northeast" (relation/11491453). The other 18 still go (5 on the 19).
- **Not done:** no map rendered, so `check_place_search.py` still fails rule A on the 19 and nothing else; wave 1's configs not edited.

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: the third rule list, from the final group's reads (owner)

- **Eighteen candidates came from the final group's reads of the Korean and
  German cities** (numbered in `docs/decisions_drafts/search-final.md` on
  branch `search-final`). Three were ruled by the owner in that session (16
  public art needs a Wikipedia article of its own; 17 a code beside a path
  type on minor ways; 18 a dead railway alignment with no article). Cleanup
  brought the rest in three groups with a recommendation each. **The owner:
  "new rules and narrow version accepted".**
- **New rules, to build:** (3) a park whose operator is an apartment estate
  or its management office goes; (4) a customers-only service road named by
  a description goes; (7) an exhibit admitted only by its class's article
  goes; (9) a relation that repeats another row's Wikidata entry far from
  the object goes; (10) a link to a list article with an anchor is no
  article of the object's own; (13) a public-transport station with no rail
  tag is no station.
- **Narrow version:** (11) a name ending "(geschlossen)", "(abgebrannt)",
  "(stillgelegt)" or "(ausser Betrieb)" in its German spelling is gone;
  "ehem." and "ehemalig" are not read (39 Berlin names, several standing).
  (15) A park tag on a pool, a club or an office gets no rule and stays a
  by-id drop.
- **Extensions of rules already approved, built with them, the owner:
  "already approved rules stay approved":** (1) Korean bare
  type words; (2) Korean numbered parks as code plus type; (8) the
  private-street rule reads living streets; (12) a Stolperschwelle is a
  Stolperstein; (14) the home-name test reads parks and gardens. Each shows
  in the changed-row report before it lands.
- **Rule 3 beside question g of this date:** a park tied to a housing
  complex only by its name stays; one whose operator tag names the estate
  goes.
- **One pass, one report:** these, the German lists and
  `PLACE_QUIET_STATIONS` are built together on branch `search-rules-3`.

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: every drawn line and station is searchable, the floor for the big review (owner)

- **The owner: "when we do our final big sweep, we should ensure all drawn
  lines and stations are searchable (bare minimum)".**
- **What answers it today:** the search box takes a map's own stations and
  lines from the map itself (`pipeline/map_common.py`, kinds `mapstation`
  and `line`), not from the OpenStreetMap index, so a drawn station is
  findable even when no index row carries it (Monterrey's Sendero, absent
  from its index on 2026-10-13, is such a case to test). Nothing checks it:
  check H measures reach around a station, not whether its name is found.
- **Owed before the big review's landing:** a check that opens every
  rendered map with search, types each drawn station's name and each drawn
  line's name as the map shows them, and fails naming any that is not
  returned as its own kind. A browser script beside
  `scripts/check_map_view.js`, run over all maps at the sweep; a failure is
  a map to fix, never a name to skip.
- The retrospectives of this date's earlier entry are private artifacts
  (owner), which supersedes the "docs file" form in PLAN.md's item.

### 2026-10-13 - Place search: "an article of its own" is asked of Wikidata, not of the map's tag; candidates 29 to 44 (owner)

- **The third pass, measured on the 19 live cities in memory (branch
  `search-rules-3`): 322,843 rows to 321,534.** Rule 16 of this date (public
  art needs a Wikipedia article of its own) dropped 1,278 rows, and among
  them works a visitor searches for: the Angel de la Independencia, the
  Burghers of Calais, Tokyo's Maman, Sue the Dinosaur, A-maze-ing Laughter,
  Prague's Miminka. Each carries a `wikidata` tag and no `wikipedia` tag:
  the tag on the map is a poor stand-in for the article.
- **Three options went to the owner** (ask Wikidata; keep the rule and keep
  the famous by id; leave public art out). **The owner: "1. yes and delegate
  to deepseek. no rule for brasilia."** A fetch script
  (`pipeline/place_wikidata_fetch.py`) asks Wikidata which entries named in
  a city's place caches have a Wikipedia article in any language and caches
  the answer; the index step reads the cache. The script is written by the
  DeepSeek worker from a plan (new file only; code, no data sent), reviewed
  and wired in by Cleanup. Wikidata is a new source: its licence row is
  read before the first request.
- **No rule for the Brasilia finding:** applying the street-word test to
  pedestrian ways would drop 483 live rows, 266 in London, many of them
  real pedestrian streets. Brasilia's corridors stay by-id drops.
- **Candidates 36 and 39, the owner: "36. declare. 39. same rule".**
  Cluj-Napoca declares Hungarian and Daugavpils Russian as searched
  languages (39.8% and 15.5% of rows). A library needs an article of its
  own, as public art does (Florence: 213 of 1,004 landmarks).
- **Built with the seven language lists as a fourth pass, on Cleanup's
  recommendation, not objected to:** (29) a leak word is set aside in a
  language where it is a plain word ("store"); (31) Greek final sigma and
  (35) Romanian cedilla letters fold; (32) `int_name` is read as an
  alternate name; (33) a glued way word passes in a suffix language; (34) a
  home-name ending; (37) Swedish "-gard" is no street word on a footway;
  (38) a Wikidata entry that is the subject's is not the object's own; (40)
  one monument drawn in segments is one row; (41) a place of worship as a
  node beside its way is one row; (42) a station closed to passengers is no
  station; (43) Cluj-Napoca's tram stops lose a trailing " Tram". (44) gets
  no rule.
- **Also from the third pass:** the station guard brings back Sendero,
  Brussels' Parc, Mexico City's La Virgen and Blackpool's Tower; the index
  holds no line rows, and lines and a map's own stations reach the search
  box from the map with no name filter (pinned by a selftest); foot access
  brings back 189 public streets; four rules were narrowed after measuring
  (the draft has each). The German matcher words are a separate commit
  (`search-rules-3-matcher`), held for the big review's render.
